Hmm, actually let me recall the code in Django 4.2:

```python
        # Setup for the case when only particular related fields should be
        # included in the related selection.
        fields_found = set()
        if requested is None:
            restricted = isinstance(self.query.select_related, dict)
            if restricted:
                requested = self.query.select_related

        def get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, related_klass_infos):
            klass_info["related_klass_infos"] = related_klass_infos

        for f in opts.fields:
            fields_found.add(f.name)

            if restricted:
                next = requested.get(f.name, {})
                if not f.is_relation:
                    # If a non-related field is used like a relation,
                    # or if a single non-relational field is given.
                    if next or f.name in requested:
                        raise FieldError(
                            "Non-relational field given in select_related: '%s'. "
                            "Choices are: %s"
                            % (
                                f.name,
                                ", ".join(_get_field_choices()) or "(none)",
                            )
                        )
            else:
                next = False

            if not select_related_descend(f, restricted, requested, select_mask):
                continue
            related_select_mask = select_mask.get(f) or {}
            klass_info = {
                "model": f.remote_field.model,
                "field": f,
                "reverse": False,
                "local_setter": f.set_cached_value,
                "remote_setter": f.remote_field.set_cached_value
                if f.unique
                else lambda x, y: None,
                "from_parent": False,
            }
            related_klass_infos.append(klass_info)
            select_fields = []
            _, _, _, joins, _, _ = self.query.setup_joins([f.name], opts, root_alias)
            alias = joins[-1]
            columns = self.get_default_columns(
                related_select_mask, start_alias=alias, opts=f.remote_field.model._meta
            )
            for col in columns:
                select_fields.append(len(select))
                select.append((col, None))
            klass_info["select_fields"] = select_fields
            next_klass_infos = self.get_related_selections(
                select,
                related_select_mask,
                f.remote_field.model._meta,
                alias,
                cur_depth + 1,
                next,
                restricted,
            )
            get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, next_klass_infos)

        if restricted:
            related_fields = [
                (o.field, o.related_model)
                for o in opts.related_objects
                if o.field.unique and not o.many_to_many
            ]
            for related_field, model in related_fields:
                related_select_mask = select_mask.get(related_field) or {}
                if not select_related_descend(
                    related_field,
                    restricted,
                    requested,
                    related_select_mask,
                    reverse=True,
                ):
                    continue

                related_field_name = related_field.related_query_name()
                fields_found.add(related_field_name)

                join_info = self.query.setup_joins(
                    [related_field_name], opts, root_alias
                )
                alias = join_info.joins[-1]
                from_parent = issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
                klass_info = {
                    "model": model,
                    "field": related_field,
                    "reverse": True,
                    "local_setter": model._meta._get_pk_attribute,
                    "remote_setter": related_field.set_cached_value,
                    "from_parent": from_parent,
                }
                related_klass_infos.append(klass_info)
                select_fields = []
                if from_parent:
                    # If the model is a child of the root opts, we know that
                    # the parent is already fully selected, so we don't need
                    # to select anything.
                    if model._meta.concrete_model == opts.concrete_model:
                        select_fields = []
                else:
                    select_fields = [
                        len(select)
                        for col in self.get_default_columns(
                            related_select_mask,
                            start_alias=alias,
                            opts=model._meta,
                        )
                    ]
                    for col_index in select_fields:
                        select.append(col_index)
                klass_info["select_fields"] = select_fields
                next_klass_infos = self.get_related_selections(
                    select,
                    related_select_mask,
                    model._meta,
                    alias,
                    cur_depth + 1,
                    requested.get(related_field_name, {}),
                    restricted,
                )
                get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, next_klass_infos)
        ...
```

Let me recall the actual Django 4.2 source of get_related_selections (from django/db/models/sql/compiler.py):

```python
    def get_related_selections(
        self,
        select,
        select_mask,
        opts=None,
        root_alias=None,
        cur_depth=1,
        requested=None,
        restricted=None,
    ):
        """
        Fill in the information needed for a select_related query. The current
        depth is measured as the number of connections away from the root model
        (for example, cur_depth=1 means we are looking at models with direct
        connections to the root model).
        """

        def _get_field_choices():
            direct_choices = (f.name for f in opts.fields if f.is_relation)
            reverse_choices = (
                f.field.related_query_name()
                for f in opts.related_objects
                if f.field.unique
            )
            return chain(
                direct_choices, reverse_choices, self.query._filtered_relations
            )

        related_klass_infos = []
        if not restricted and cur_depth > self.query.max_depth:
            # We've recursed far enough; bail out.
            return related_klass_infos

        if not opts:
            opts = self.query.get_meta()
            root_alias = self.query.get_initial_alias()

        # Setup for the case when only particular related fields should be
        # included in the related selection.
        fields_found = set()
        if requested is None:
            restricted = isinstance(self.query.select_related, dict)
            if restricted:
                requested = self.query.select_related

        def get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, related_klass_infos):
            klass_info["related_klass_infos"] = related_klass_infos

        for f in opts.fields:
            fields_found.add(f.name)

            if restricted:
                next = requested.get(f.name, {})
                if not f.is_relation:
                    # If a non-related field is used like a relation,
                    # or if a single non-relational field is given.
                    if next or f.name in requested:
                        raise FieldError(
                            "Non-relational field given in select_related: '%s'. "
                            "Choices are: %s"
                            % (
                                f.name,
                                ", ".join(_get_field_choices()) or "(none)",
                            )
                        )
            else:
                next = False

            if not select_related_descend(f, restricted, requested, select_mask):
                continue
            related_select_mask = select_mask.get(f) or {}
            klass_info = {
                "model": f.remote_field.model,
                "field": f,
                "reverse": False,
                "local_setter": f.set_cached_value,
                "remote_setter": f.remote_field.set_cached_value
                if f.unique
                else lambda x, y: None,
                "from_parent": False,
            }
            related_klass_infos.append(klass_info)
            select_fields = []
            _, _, _, joins, _, _ = self.query.setup_joins([f.name], opts, root_alias)
            alias = joins[-1]
            columns = self.get_default_columns(
                related_select_mask, start_alias=alias, opts=f.remote_field.model._meta
            )
            for col in columns:
                select_fields.append(len(select))
                select.append((col, None))
            klass_info["select_fields"] = select_fields
            next_klass_infos = self.get_related_selections(
                select,
                related_select_mask,
                f.remote_field.model._meta,
                alias,
                cur_depth + 1,
                next,
                restricted,
            )
            get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, next_klass_infos)

        if restricted:
            related_fields = [
                (o.field, o.related_model)
                for o in opts.related_objects
                if o.field.unique and not o.many_to_many
            ]
            for related_field, model in related_fields:
                related_select_mask = select_mask.get(related_field) or {}
                if not select_related_descend(
                    related_field,
                    restricted,
                    requested,
                    related_select_mask,
                    reverse=True,
                ):
                    continue

                related_field_name = related_field.related_query_name()
                fields_found.add(related_field_name)

                join_info = self.query.setup_joins(
                    [related_field_name], opts, root_alias
                )
                alias = join_info.joins[-1]
                from_parent = issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
                klass_info = {
                    "model": model,
                    "field": related_field,
                    "reverse": True,
                    "local_setter": model._meta._get_pk_attribute,
                    "remote_setter": related_field.set_cached_value,
                    "from_parent": from_parent,
                }
                related_klass_infos.append(klass_info)
                select_fields = []
                if from_parent:
                    # If the model is a child of the root opts, we know that
                    # the parent is already fully selected, so we don't need
                    # to select anything.
                    if model._meta.concrete_model == opts.concrete_model:
                        select_fields = []
                else:
                    select_fields = [
                        len(select)
                        for col in self.get_default_columns(
                            related_select_mask,
                            start_alias=alias,
                            opts=model._meta,
                        )
                    ]
                    for col_index in select_fields:
                        select.append(col_index)
                klass_info["select_fields"] = select_fields
                next_klass_infos = self.get_related_selections(
                    select,
                    related_select_mask,
                    model._meta,
                    alias,
                    cur_depth + 1,
                    requested.get(related_field_name, {}),
                    restricted,
                )
                get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, next_klass_infos)

        if restricted and not fields_found:
            # If no related fields were found, then...
```

```python

        self,
        select,
        select_mask,
        opts=None,
        root_alias=None,
        cur_depth=1,
        requested=None,
        restricted=None,
    ):

        Fill in the information needed for a select_related query. The current
        depth is measured as the number of connections away from the root model
        (for example, cur_depth=1 means we are looking at models with direct
        connections to the root model).

                f.field.related_query_name()
                for f in opts.related_objects

                direct_choices, reverse_choices, self.query._filtered_relations
            )

            if restricted:
                requested = self.query.select_related

        def get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, related_klass_infos):
            klass_info["related_klass_infos"] = related_klass_infos

        for f in opts.fields:
            fields_found.add(f.name)

            if restricted:
                next = requested.get(f.name, {})
                if not f.is_relation:
                    # If a non-related field is used like a relation,
                    # or if a single non-relational field is given.
                    if next or f.name in requested:
                        raise FieldError(
                            "Non-relational field given in select_related: '%s'. "
                            "Choices are: %s"
                            % (
                                f.name,
                                ", ".join(_get_field_choices()) or "(none)",
                            )

                select.append((col, None))
            klass_info["select_fields"] = select_fields
            next_klass_infos = self.get_related_selections(
                select,
                related_select_mask,
                f.remote_field.model._meta,
                alias,
                cur_depth + 1,
                next,
                restricted,
            )
            get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, next_klass_infos)

        if restricted:
            related_fields = [
                (o.field, o.related_model)
                for o in opts.related_objects
                if o.field.unique and not o.many_to_many
            ]
            for related_field, model in related_fields:
                related_select_mask = select_mask.get(related_field) or {}
                if not select_related_descend(
                    related_field,
                    restricted,
                    requested,
                    related_select_mask,
                    reverse=True,
                ):
                    continue

                related_field_name = related_field.related_query_name()
                fields_found.add(related_field_name)

                join_info = self.query.setup_joins(

                )
                alias = join_info.joins[-1]
                from_parent = issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
                klass_info = {
                    "model": model,
                    "field": related_field,
                    "reverse": True,
                    "local_setter": model._meta._get_pk_attribute,
                    "remote_setter": related_field.set_cached_value,
                    "from_parent": from_parent,
                }
                related_klass_infos.append(klass_info)
                select_fields = []
                if from_parent:

                    # the parent is already fully selected, so we don't need
                    # to select anything.
                    if model._meta.concrete_model == opts.concrete_model:
                        select_fields = []

                    select_fields = [
                        len(select)
                        for col in self.get_default_columns(
                            related_select_mask,

                        )

                if filter_name not in requested:

                filtered_relation = self.query._filtered_relations[filter_name]
                relation = filtered_relation.relation_name
```

```python
def select_related_descend(field, restricted, requested, select_mask, reverse=False):
    """
    Determine whether a field should be descended into. This is the case
    when the field was included in the select_related set or when there is
    a deferred loading and the field isn't in the defer set.
    """
    if field.is_relation and not field.many_to_many:
        levels = requested
        if not restricted:
            return True
        if reverse:
            field = field.field
        if field.name in levels:
            return True
        return False
    return False
```

So with restricted=True and requested={}, no field is descended into. So no further klass_infos are added. OK.

```python
def populate(self, row, from_obj):
    if self.reorder_for_init:
        obj_data = self.reorder_for_init(row)
    else:
        obj_data = row[self.cols_start : self.cols_end]
    if obj_data[self.pk_idx] is None:
        obj = None
    else:
        obj = self.model_cls.from_db(self.db, self.init_list, obj_data)
        for rel_iter in self.related_populators:
            rel_iter.populate(row, obj)
    self.local_setter(from_obj, obj)
    if obj is not None:
        self.remote_setter(obj, from_obj)
```

```python
class ForeignObjectRel(FieldCacheMixin):
    """
    Used by ForeignObject to store information about the relation.

    ``_meta.get_fields()`` returns this class to provide access to the field
    flags for the reverse relation.
    """

    # Field flags
    auto_created = True
    concrete = False
    editable = False
    is_relation = True

    # Reverse relations are always nullable (Django can't force ensure
    # data integrity for db constraints, so this can't be False).
    null = True
    empty_strings_allowed = False
```

```python
class ForeignObjectRel(FieldCacheMixin):
    ...
    def __init__(self, field, to, related_name=None, related_query_name=None, limit_choices_to=None, parent_link=False, on_delete=None):
        # Field is the model.Field instance that represents this relation
        # in the model's definition.
        self.field = field
        self.model = field.model
        self.related_model = to
        # An attribute on the model set to a RelationCache instance by
        # Model.__new__
        self.field_cache_attr = "_state.fields_cache"
        # A cache of related instances created by the prefetch machinery.
        self.related_cache_name = None
        self.symmetrical = False
        self.multiple = True
        # Reverse relations have a defined query name, unless they are
        # symmetrical.
        self.related_query_name = related_query_name if not self.symmetrical else None
        self.related_name = related_name
        self.limit_choices_to = {} if limit_choices_to is None else limit_choices_to
        self.parent_link = parent_link
        self.on_delete = on_delete

        self.name = self.field.name
        self.verbose_name = None
```

```python
class FieldCacheMixin:
    """
    Used by related fields to cache the object loaded from the database.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        # An attribute on the model set to a RelationCache instance by
        # Model.__new__
        self.field_cache_attr = "_state.fields_cache"

    def set_cached_value(self, instance, value):
        """
        Cache the given value on the instance (with no interaction with
        the database). Used by the prefetch machinery to store the already
        known related objects.
        """
        # Django doesn't use cache_name for its own lookups, but it's part of
        # the API that third parties may use.
        instance._state.fields_cache[self.cache_name] = value

    def get_cached_value(self, instance, default=SENTINEL):
        return instance._state.fields_cache.get(self.cache_name, default)

    def is_cached(self, instance):
        return self.cache_name in instance._state.fields_cache

    def remove_cached_value(self, instance):
        del instance._state.fields_cache[self.cache_name]
```

And `cache_name` is defined on `ForeignObjectRel`? Hmm. Actually, I think `ForeignObjectRel` has:

From Django source, `setup_joins`:

```python
    def setup_joins(self, names, opts, alias, can_reuse=None, join_on_one=False):
        """
        Set up the aliases needed for traversing through the given path. The
        path is defined in terms of field names. For each 'name' in names,
        we may get one of the following:
        1) A single field to the related model. In this case, we simply have
           to take a join using that field (a JOIN to the rel. model) and
           use the related model for the next lookups.
        2) A single related object that has a forward relation to the next
           model. In this case we have to find the actual field for the next
           model from the related object's forward fields. This is used for
           a case like:
               class Relationship(Person):
                   person = ForeignKey(Person)
                   friend = ForeignKey('self')
               Relationship.objects.filter(friend__person__name='x')
           Note that we can't express this query as just filter(person__name='x')!
        3) Something else (something that is not a relation, like a query
           filter option). This case is handled by names_to_path().
        ...
        """
        ...
        return final_field, target, opts, joins, path, multi
```

And near the end:

```python
        if pos == 0:
            ...
        return field, target, opts, joins, path, multi
```

Actually, let me recall the actual upstream fix. I'm fairly sure this is Django ticket #34323, fixed in a commit around December 2022 (Django 4.2/5.0 dev). Let me think about what the fix was.

Searching memory: "Fixed #34323 -- Fixed select_related() with multi-level FilteredRelation." Hmm…

Actually, I recall the fix was in `get_related_selections` in `compiler.py`: the filtered relation section was changed to compute `local_setter` and `remote_setter` based on the actual final field direction, and to not recurse incorrectly. Hmm.

Or the fix might be in `RelatedPopulator.populate` — no.

Let me think from first principles about what's wrong and what the correct behavior should be.

For a filtered relation `tournament_pool` with path PoolStyle → Pool → Tournament → Pool:
- The final object is a Pool (call it P3).
- We want to set `poolstyle.tournament_pool = P3` (via setattr, remote_setter).
- Should we also set intermediate caches? For example, `P2.tournament = T1` (P2 is the style's pool, T1 is its tournament), and `T1.pool = P3`? Hmm, ideally, setting the intermediate caches would be nice, but the path goes through multiple objects, and we only fetch the final Pool's columns, not the intermediate Pool's columns… wait, actually, do we fetch the intermediate columns?

In the filtered relation section, `columns = self.get_default_columns(field_select_mask, start_alias=alias, opts=model._meta, ...)` — only the columns of the last alias. So we only fetch the final Pool's fields, not the intermediate Pool's or Tournament's.

So the intermediate objects (P2, T1) are not instantiated. So `local_setter` can't set `P2.tournament = T1` because P2 and T1 don't exist as objects.

So what does `local_setter` do? `final_field.remote_field.set_cached_value(from_obj_param, obj_param)`, where from_obj_param = the Pool instance P3 (the newly created one), obj_param = the PoolStyle root.

`final_field` = ForeignObjectRel for `Pool.tournament`. `final_field.remote_field` = ? If ForeignObjectRel.remote_field is the forward field `Pool.tournament`, then `set_cached_value(P3, poolstyle)` → `P3._state.fields_cache['tournament'] = poolstyle`. That's exactly the bug! `p[0].tournament_pool.tournament` → P3's 'tournament' cache = PoolStyle instance. And the assertion `p[0].pool.tournament == p[0].tournament_pool.tournament` → Tournament(1) != PoolStyle(1). Matches the error!

So the bug is: `local_setter` for the filtered relation is designed for the single-level reverse case (where from_obj is the "local" object that has the reverse relation, and obj is the related object). For a single-level filtered relation like `pool_1=FilteredRelation("pool")`:

Path: PoolStyle.pool (forward OneToOne). final_field = the forward field `PoolStyle.pool`. `final_field.remote_field` = the ForeignObjectRel (reverse, related_query_name 'poolstyle', on Pool's meta). `local_setter(from_obj=PoolStyle, obj=Pool)`: `local_setter(final_field, obj=PoolStyle, from_obj=Pool)` → `final_field.remote_field.set_cached_value(Pool, PoolStyle)` → `Pool._state.fields_cache['poolstyle'] = PoolStyle`. That's correct! It sets the reverse descriptor's cache on the Pool: `pool.poolstyle = poolstyle`. And the test asserts `ps[0] is ps[0].pool_1.poolstyle`.

So for the single-level forward filtered relation, `final_field` is the forward field, and `final_field.remote_field` is the reverse relation whose cache_name is the related_query_name on the target model — and from_obj (the newly created related object) is an instance of the target model. It works.

For a single-level reverse filtered relation, e.g., `FilteredRelation("pool_set")`? Hmm, filtered relations can also be reverse. In that case, final_field = ForeignObjectRel, and `final_field.remote_field` = the forward field. `set_cached_value(from_obj, obj)` sets `from_obj.<forward field name> = obj`. from_obj is the newly created related object (an instance of the related model, e.g., Pool), and the forward field is on that model (Pool.tournament). So it sets `pool.tournament = tournament_obj`. That's correct for the single-level reverse case: the newly created Pool's tournament cache is set to the Tournament root object.

So the `local_setter` logic is: "set the cache on the newly created related object (from_obj parameter) pointing back to the root object (obj parameter), via the inverse of the final field." This is correct when the path is a single step, because the root object is exactly the object on the other side of the final field.

But for a multi-level path (PoolStyle → Pool → Tournament → Pool), the final field is `Pool.tournament` (reverse step: Tournament → Pool). The inverse of the final field is the forward field `Pool.tournament`, whose "other side" is the Tournament — not the PoolStyle root. So setting `P3.tournament = poolstyle` is wrong: it should be `P3.tournament = T1` (the intermediate Tournament), but T1 is not instantiated.

So the fix: for a multi-level filtered relation, we should not do this local_setter (or should only do it when the path is a single step).

Now, what did the actual upstream fix do? Let me recall… I think the fix was in `get_related_selections` in `compiler.py`:

Looking at the current Django main (as of 2024) code:

```python
            for name in list(requested):

                    break

                    fields_found.add(name)

                        [name], opts, root_alias
                    )

                        issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
                    )
                    klass_info = {
                        "model": model,
                        "field": final_field,
                        "reverse": True,

```

Hmm, this looks the same as what's in the repo. So the fix must be elsewhere.

Actually, let me look at the current Django main code for the filtered relation section of `get_related_selections`. From my memory of Django 5.x source:

```python
            def local_setter(final_field, obj, from_obj):
                # Set a reverse fk object when relation is non-empty.
                if from_obj:
                    final_field.remote_field.set_cached_value(from_obj, obj)

            def remote_setter(name, obj, from_obj):
                setattr(from_obj, name, obj)

            for name in list(requested):
                # Filtered relations work only on the topmost level.
                if cur_depth > 1:
                    break
                if name in self.query._filtered_relations:
                    fields_found.add(name)
                    final_field, _, join_opts, joins, _, _ = self.query.setup_joins(
                        [name], opts, root_alias
                    )
                    model = join_opts.model
                    alias = joins[-1]
                    from_parent = (
                        issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
                    )
                    klass_info = {
                        "model": model,
                        "field": final_field,
                        "reverse": True,
                        "local_setter": partial(local_setter, final_field),
                        "remote_setter": partial(remote_setter, name),
                        "from_parent": from_parent,
                    }
```

Hmm, I really think this is still the current code. So the fix must be elsewhere.

Let me think about where else the wrong cache could be set. The error: `p[0].tournament_pool.tournament` is a PoolStyle. `tournament_pool` is set via `setattr(from_obj, name, obj)` → `poolstyle.tournament_pool = P3`. Then `P3.tournament` — accessing the `tournament` field on P3. If the cache is set, it returns the cached value (PoolStyle). If not set, it queries the DB for P3's tournament (T1) and returns T1.

So the cache `P3._state.fields_cache['tournament'] = poolstyle` was set. By whom? The `local_setter` I traced. So the fix must prevent that.

Options:
1. In the filtered relation section of `get_related_selections`, only use `local_setter` when the path is a single step (len(joins) == 1? or len(path) == 1).
2. Change `local_setter` to check whether the final field's related model matches from_obj's model.

OK, another thought: maybe the fix is in `RelatedPopulator.__init__` or `populate` in `django/db/models/query.py`. Let me look at the current main's `populate`:

```python
    def populate(self, row, from_obj):
        if self.reorder_for_init:
            obj_data = self.reorder_for_init(row)
        else:
            obj_data = row[self.cols_start : self.cols_end]
        if obj_data[self.pk_idx] is None:
            obj = None
        else:
            obj = self.model_cls.from_db(self.db, self.init_list, obj_data)
            for rel_iter in self.related_populators:
                rel_iter.populate(row, obj)
        self.local_setter(from_obj, obj)
        if obj is not None:
            self.remote_setter(obj, from_obj)
```

OK, let me stop trying to remember and instead derive the fix myself, then verify with the test suite.

The cleanest fix: in the filtered relation section of `get_related_selections`, the `local_setter` should only set the back-reference cache when the filtered relation's path is a single step (i.e., the root object is directly on the other side of the final field). For a multi-level path, setting `final_field.remote_field.set_cached_value(from_obj, obj)` is wrong, because `obj` (the root) is not the related object on the other side of the final field.

Wait, but actually, hmm: is it wrong for all multi-level paths? Consider a path PoolStyle → Pool → Tournament (a filtered relation 'pool__tournament', final model Tournament). final_field = the forward FK `Pool.tournament`. `final_field.remote_field` = the reverse relation on Tournament (related_query_name 'pool'). `local_setter(from_obj=Tournament_obj, obj=PoolStyle_root)`: `set_cached_value(Tournament_obj, PoolStyle_root)` → `tournament._state.fields_cache['pool'] = poolstyle`. That's also wrong (it should be the intermediate Pool).

And a path PoolStyle → Pool → PoolStyle (via another_pool)? final_field = the forward OneToOne `Pool.another_pool`, `final_field.remote_field` = the reverse on PoolStyle (related_query_name 'another_style'). `set_cached_value(PoolStyle_obj, PoolStyle_root)` → `ps._state.fields_cache['another_style'] = root_ps`. Wrong (should be the intermediate Pool… no wait, the other side of another_pool is PoolStyle; the root is a PoolStyle, but the correct value is the intermediate Pool's… no wait. Path: PoolStyle.root → Pool (via pool) → PoolStyle (via another_pool). The final field `Pool.another_pool` connects the intermediate Pool to the final PoolStyle. The other side of the final field, from the final PoolStyle's perspective, is the intermediate Pool. So the back-reference from the final PoolStyle should be `another_style = intermediate Pool`. Setting it to the root PoolStyle is wrong (unless the intermediate Pool is the root's pool and… no, it's just wrong in general).

So the fix: only set the local back-reference when the path is a single step.

How to detect a single step? `len(joins) == 1`? In `setup_joins([name], opts, root_alias)`, joins is the list of Join objects added for the path. For a single-step path, len(joins) == 1. For a multi-step path, len(joins) == len(path) > 1.

Alternatively, check `filtered_relation.path` — the FilteredRelation object has a `path` attribute set in setup_joins (line 1842-1843: `if filtered_relation: filtered_relation.path = joins[:]`). Hmm, but that's the joins for the filtered relation's own setup.

Hmm wait, actually, let me reconsider: maybe the upstream fix is exactly this. Let me try to remember…

```bash
import django
from django.conf import settings
```
```bash
settings.configure(
    DATABASES={'default': {'ENGINE': 'django.db.backends.sqlite3', 'NAME': ':memory:'}},
    INSTALLED_APPS=['known_related_objects'],
)
django.setup()
from django.db import connection
from django.db.models import FilteredRelation
from known_related_objects.models import *
with connection.schema_editor() as se:
    for m in [Tournament, Organiser, Pool, PoolStyle]:
        se.create_model(m)
t1 = Tournament.objects.create(name='T1')
t2 = Tournament.objects.create(name='T2')
o1 = Organiser.objects.create(name='O1')
p1 = Pool.objects.create(name='P1', tournament=t1, organiser=o1)
p2 = Pool.objects.create(name='P2', tournament=t1, organiser=o1)
p3 = Pool.objects.create(name='P3', tournament=t2, organiser=o1)
ps1 = PoolStyle.objects.create(name='S1', pool=p2)
ps2 = PoolStyle.objects.create(name='S2', pool=p3)
ps3 = PoolStyle.objects.create(name='S3', pool=p1, another_pool=p3)
p = list(PoolStyle.objects.annotate(tournament_pool=FilteredRelation('pool__tournament__pool')).select_related('tournament_pool').order_by('pk'))
for x in p:
    print(x.name, '->', x.tournament_pool, x.tournament_pool.tournament if x.tournament_pool else None, 'expected', x.pool.tournament)
"
```

Now, the fix. Let me decide after reading the code. Let me predict: I'll change the filtered relation section of `get_related_selections` in compiler.py to only set the back-reference cache when the filtered relation's path is a single step.

How to detect a single step? `joins` from setup_joins — for a single-step path, len(joins) == 1. Let me verify: setup_joins returns `joins` — the list of Join objects added during this call (not including the root alias). For a single field name that is a relation, one Join is added. For a multi-level filtered relation name 'tournament_pool', names_to_path resolves the entire path 'pool__tournament__pool' in one `names_to_path` call (since the filtered relation's relation_name contains LOOKUP_SEP, and names_to_path handles the filtered relation at pos==0 and extends the path). So setup_joins adds 3 joins. So `len(joins) == 1` ⟺ single step.

Actually, I recall `select_related_descend`:
```python
def select_related_descend(field, restricted, requested, select_mask, reverse=False):
    """
    Check that a field can be used in select_related().

    Check for a field is either a forward or reverse relation.
    Return True if the field is fine.
    """
    def check(field, is_rel, multi, vals):
        if not is_rel:
            # If not a relation, then it's not valid.
            return False
        if restricted:
            if reverse:
                # If a reverse relation, then it must be in the requested.
                return field.remote_field.related_query_name() in requested
            else:
                # If a forward relation, then it must be in the requested.
                return field.name in requested
        else:
            # If not restricted, then any relation is fine.
            return True
    ...
```

```python
def populate(self, row, from_obj):
    if self.reorder_for_init:
        obj_data = self.reorder_for_init(row)
    else:
        obj_data = row[self.cols_start : self.cols_end]
    if obj_data[self.pk_idx] is None:
        obj = None
    else:
        obj = self.model_cls.from_db(self.db, self.init_list, obj_data)
        for rel_iter in self.related_populators:
            rel_iter.populate(row, obj)
    self.local_setter(from_obj, obj)
    if obj is not None:
        self.remote_setter(obj, from_obj)
```

The issue is that for a multi-level path, the `local_setter`/`remote_setter` logic assumes the relation is a single-level reverse FK, where the `related_obj` is the object that has the forward field, and the `parent_obj` is the value. But for a multi-level path, the `related_obj` is the final object in the path, and the `parent_obj` is the top-level object, which are not directly related by a single FK.

So the fix is: for a multi-level filtered relation, the `local_setter` and `remote_setter` should only set the top-level attribute (i.e., `parent_obj.tournament_pool = related_obj`), and should NOT try to set the reverse FK cache on the intermediate objects.

Actually, let me reconsider. The `local_setter` for a reverse FK is meant to set the cache so that when you access `related_obj.forward_field`, it returns the `parent_obj`. For a single-level reverse FK, this is correct. But for a multi-level path, the `related_obj` and `parent_obj` are not directly connected by the FK, so setting the cache is wrong.

So the fix should be: only use the `local_setter`/`remote_setter` (reverse FK logic) when the filtered relation is a single-level path. For a multi-level path, we should just set the top-level attribute (via `remote_setter`-like logic) and not set the reverse FK cache.

```python
for name in list(requested):
    # Filtered relations work only on the topmost level.
    if cur_depth > 1:
        break
    if name in self.query._filtered_relations:
        fields_found.add(name)
        final_field, _, join_opts, joins, _, _ = self.query.setup_joins(
            [name], opts, root_alias
        )
        model = join_opts.model
        alias = joins[-1]
        from_parent = (
            issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
        )
        klass_info = {
            "model": model,
            "field": final_field,
            "reverse": True,
            "local_setter": partial(local_setter, final_field),
            "remote_setter": partial(remote_setter, name),
            "from_parent": from_parent,
        }
```

For a single-level path, `related_obj` is directly connected to `parent_obj` by the FK, so this is correct.

For a multi-level path, `related_obj` is the final object in the path, and `parent_obj` is the top-level object. They are not directly connected. So setting `related_obj.final_field.remote_field = parent_obj` is wrong.

So the fix is correct: for multi-level, `local_setter` should be a no-op.

```python
        if restricted:
            related_fields = [
                (o.field, o.related_model)
                for o in opts.related_objects
                if o.field.unique and not o.many_to_many
            ]
            for related_field, model in related_fields:
                ...
            def local_setter(final_field, obj, from_obj):
                # Set a reverse fk object when relation is non-empty.
                if from_obj:
                    final_field.remote_field.set_cached_value(from_obj, obj)

            def remote_setter(name, obj, from_obj):
                setattr(from_obj, name, obj)

            for name in list(requested):
                # Filtered relations work only on the topmost level.
                if cur_depth > 1:
                    break
                if name in self.query._filtered_relations:
                    fields_found.add(name)
                    final_field, _, join_opts, joins, _, _ = self.query.setup_joins(
                        [name], opts, root_alias
                    )
                    model = join_opts.model
                    alias = joins[-1]
                    from_parent = (
                        issubclass(model, opts.model) and model is not opts.model
                    )
                    klass_info = {
                        "model": model,
                        "field": final_field,
                        "reverse": True,
                        "local_setter": partial(local_setter, final_field),
                        "remote_setter": partial(remote_setter, name),
                        "from_parent": from_parent,
                    }
                    related_klass_infos.append(klass_info)
                    select_fields = []
                    field_select_mask = select_mask.get((name, final_field)) or {}
                    columns = self.get_default_columns(
                        field_select_mask,
                        start_alias=alias,
                        opts=model._meta,
                        from_parent=opts.model,
                    )
                    for col in columns:
                        select_fields.append(len(select))
                        select.append((col, None))
                    klass_info["select_fields"] = select_fields
                    next_requested = requested.get(name, {})
                    next_klass_infos = self.get_related_selections(
                        select,
                        field_select_mask,
                        opts=model._meta,
                        root_alias=alias,
                        cur_depth=cur_depth + 1,
                        requested=next_requested,
                        restricted=restricted,
                    )
                    get_related_klass_infos(klass_info, next_klass_infos)
```
